Currently, the Black King is situated on light square `c6`. Let's look at the available checks:
1. **Bb5+** (`c4b5`): Checks the king, but the `b5` square appears totally unprotected, allowing the Black King to simply gobble up the piece with `...Kxb5`.
2. **Qc7+** (`f7c7`): Places the Queen on `c7`, vertically checking the `c6` King. Though White's Knight on `d5` excellently protects `c7`, Black's Queen on `d8` is unpinned and stationed precisely to capture diagonally via `...Qxc7`. 
3. **Qd7+** (`f7d7`): Unprotected square; Black simply takes it, again throwing the game.
4. **Ne7+** (`d5e7`): Checks, but the square is heavily contested by Black's `d8` Queen and `f8` Bishop.

This brings us to:
5. **Nb4+** (`d5b4`): This immediately checks the King from a safe, unassailable square. 
Let's see Black's responses to `1. Nb4+`:
The King is blocked from `c5` and `d6` by its own pawns. The `d5` square (just vacated by our Knight) is strictly covered by White's `e4` pawn and `c4` Bishop. The `b5` square is sliced by the `c4` Bishop. Squares like `c7` and `d7` remain firmly in the grip of the White Queen on `f7`. 

Black has only one legal forced escape: `...Kb6`. By driving the vulnerable Black King further into the open onto the `b6` square, you force a severe king hunt where Black is desperately devoid of cover, and you prepare lethal mating nets (with threats of pawn storms or coordinating Queen moves on the next turn).

By moving your Rook from e1 to b1, you slide it right next to the Black King, delivering a direct check.
- The Black King cannot capture the b1 Rook because it is defended by your other Rook on a1.
- The King cannot evade to the a-file (a3 is covered by the Queen on a5, and a2/a1 are occupied).
- The King cannot move to c3 because it is on the path of your Queen's diagonal (a5 to e1).
- The King cannot escape down to c1 because the newly moved Rook on b1 sweeps across the first rank as well.
- The Knight on c2 blocks the other potential escape square.

With no legal escape squares and no pieces that can capture on b1, this results in checkmate.
